# Review — 020-code-review

## Summary

The window runs from 020's reopening (`37c8219c`) to HEAD; 020's last review, 2026-10-16, predates it. One agent ran all five passes, reading all nine loaded backend and cross rule files whole. It read 19 of the 52 scope entries whole: 020's spec, plan, data model, tasks and the three scenarios in scope (and the other two scenarios, outside the scope, as contract), the command and its reference, the review-basic golden, AGENTS.md and the eight loaded rule files in scope. In depth it read the hunks no committed review had read: `c1a99ee4` (task 14), `efe81579` (task 15), `c433ad78`, `b82c1c86`, `8815e460`, `07d46995`, and the two commits naming no spec, `2604a6f9`'s AGENTS.md hunk and `b1d319f1`'s primitives.rs hunk. The other entries are unread: interpreter/mod.rs, payload.rs, extensions.rs, schema/primitives.rs, CHANGELOG.md and security-frontend.md were read only at those hunks and the functions they touch; the command and reference mirrors were checked against their sources, the generator's substitutions aside, and not read; and the rest were read in depth by committed reviews of the specs their commits name (060 for `c2d67cdc` and `48ae4b14`; 008, 017, 026, 047, 058 and 034 to 039 for the rule-file batches), so they were not opened. No loaded rule fired. Of eleven observations, eight were fixed in the run: the `passes_run` doc comment and the dead `pass` context fallback in payload.rs, three artifact corrections (the 079 signpost, the plan's criteria count and Affected Files, the data model's 079 fields), and a broken contract, §Passes in a subagent briefing no §Load rules or Observations rules; and fix round 1's NOTE, two walker tests still seeding a context `pass` nothing reads; three were waived by rule.

2 fix round(s): 2 re-read, 0 diff-checked, 0 exempt, 0 not re-read.

## Observations

- bug — the doc comment on `passes_run` says a pass a dimension-restricting flag skips is absent from the procedure, so absent from `passes-run`. `ductus exec` never reads its `args` (`run_exec` in `runtime/src/main.rs` takes them and uses none), and 022's scenario `review-exec-wiring` states the walker walks every step with the dimension-flag skipping left to the host, so under exec the procedure always holds all five `performReview` steps and `passes-run` is always all five. The comment claims a restriction nothing implements, and `framework/reference/review.md` §Security pass reads `passes-run` as the passes the run performs. — `runtime/src/interpreter/payload.rs:317` — **fixed** (decided by the rule)
- other — dead branch: `build_perform_review_request` falls back to `context.get("pass")` when the step prose names no pass. Nothing in production inserts a `pass` key into the walker context (c1a99ee4's own message says the field "was always empty"), and every `performReview` step of `framework/commands/review.md` names its pass in bold, so the `or_else` is reached only by tests that seed it. — `runtime/src/interpreter/payload.rs:354` — **fixed** (decided by the rule)
- convention — spec.md's 079 signpost still says a reopening with no code, scenario or data-model change owes no review. `compute-review-scope`'s description (081) and `framework/reference/review.md` §Window and coverage say a changed scenario or data model owes an analysis and no review, so the signpost contradicts the code and 020's own reference. — `specs/020-code-review/spec.md:47` — **fixed** (decided by the rule)
- other — plan.md says `Of the 13 acceptance criteria`, and the spec holds sixteen. — `specs/020-code-review/plan.md:70` — **fixed** (decided by the rule)
- other — plan.md's Affected Files table omits the files the deliverable now lives in: `framework/reference/review.md`, the runtime files its own §Rule files map to passes decision names, and `framework/rules/security-frontend.md`. Since 079 a review's scope for a first review reads the plan's Affected Files, so the table understates the scope. — `specs/020-code-review/plan.md:80` — **fixed** (decided by the rule)
- other — data-model.md describes the `review.md` record as written in the order below on every run and its body sections as always present, but `write-review` also always writes `contract-breaks`, writes `excluded`, `open-findings` and `fix-rounds-open` when set, and renders `## Excluded from scope`, none of which its field table, YAML example or body-section table list. 079's data model defines them; 020's names only 078's fix-round fields and 081's `unread`. — `specs/020-code-review/data-model.md:105` — **fixed** (decided by the rule)
- bug — `framework/reference/review.md` §Passes in a subagent briefs the subagent with the feature, scope, loaded rule files, flag, that section, the five pass sections and the `write-review` shapes. It does not name §Load rules, which holds the rule-file-to-pass mapping task 14 added and which the Security pass section points at, nor the Observations rules under §Write `review.md`, which the pass sections point at for what is an observation and what is not. Neither the efficiency nor the quality pass section says it applies a rule file, so a subagent briefed exactly as the section states is never told which loaded file its pass applies, and the unapplied-file naming and Not-flagged routing rest on sections it was not given. On the subagent path the scenario every-loaded-rule-file-has-a-pass guarantee therefore depends on a brief the reference does not specify. Medium confidence: read from the text, not driven with a subagent. — `framework/reference/review.md:121` (breaks the spec's contract) — **fixed** (decided by the rule)
- other — the Context of scenarios every-loaded-rule-file-has-a-pass and review-reports-a-defect-under-its-owner states in the present tense what task 14 and task 15 changed: that the reference names a rule file in only one pass, that the quality pass's definition does not say it enforces `quality-cross.md`, that `/review` applies no Not-flagged clause, and that 18 yields sit in files no pass applies today. — `specs/020-code-review/scenarios/every-loaded-rule-file-has-a-pass.md:9` — **waived**: Each Context records the defect as found on a stated date (2026-10-19, 2026-11-02, anchored to ab2ad085 in the Resolved Questions), the Resolved Questions and Behavior state the requirement, and rewording the found-state changes no requirement. (decided by the rule)
- other — plan.md's first Technical Decision says `/ductus:review` has no new code and no new runtime, and its Overview says the same, while the plan's later decisions record the runtime half (`process-waivers`, `review_pass_for_rule_file`) and §Plan records the design as it stands. — `specs/020-code-review/plan.md:9` — **waived**: Rewriting the Overview and the first decision into the runtime-backed design is a design-record edit, not a mechanical chore, and the plan's later decisions state the runtime half. (decided by the rule)
- other — the unapplied-file naming in the Summary on a restricted run rests on the reviewer writing it: `write-review`'s `## Skipped passes` lists passes only, and nothing derives the rule files the skipped passes would have applied, although `review_pass_for_rule_file` maps them. — `framework/commands/review.md:125` — **waived**: The record's `skipped-passes` plus the §Load rules mapping let a reader derive the unapplied files, so the record is not indistinguishable from a clean one; deriving them in write-review is new runtime requirement, not a chore. (decided by the rule)
- other — two walker tests still seeded a context `pass` key that nothing reads once the fallback went, and their step prose named no pass in bold, so their requests asked a pass of no name (fix round 1's re-read) — `runtime/tests/walker.rs` — **fixed** (decided by the rule)
